### Phase 4 crash and the GPU overclock -- 2026-11-11 (review session, outside the editor)

- 2026-11-11 17:41:54, Unreal Editor 5.9.0 stopped with `Assertion failed: NumUVs > 0`, `StaticMesh.cpp` line 10252, 70 seconds after Phase 4 began (journal line K1, commit `219e4784`). Crash record: `D:\Unreal_Engine_Projects\Wieland1527 5.9\Saved\Crashes\UECC-Windows-DDE47498482204976AE531921BA3BF98_0002`. The log's last lines show an `execute_python` call building a surroundings mesh through Geometry Script, with repeated `AppendBuffersToMesh: Triangle cannot be added because it would create invalid Non-Manifold Mesh Topology`, then the assertion while the static mesh asset was being built. Cause, confirmed in code: `_make_mesh` in `docs/unreal/1529_wieland/tools/wieland_surroundings.py` makes each mesh from vertices and triangles only and never adds a UV channel; an Unreal static mesh must have at least one, so the first new asset fails. Not a GPU or memory fault. Fix (for the session that resumes Phase 4): after `append_buffers_to_mesh`, give the mesh one UV channel and fill it (for example `unreal.GeometryScript_UVs.set_num_uv_channels(mesh, 1)` then a box projection), and test one file before a batch; also count the triangles the append rejected as non-manifold, because those are holes in the generated buildings. Status: resolved on 2026-11-11 at 20:04 by the session that resumed Phase 4. Fix applied in `_make_mesh`: UV channel 0 is filled per vertex in the buffers and then by a 1 m box projection (`set_mesh_u_vs_from_box_projection`), triangles without UVs are counted, and no asset is made from a mesh that has no UV channel. Rejected triangles, counted in the editor by appending every file to a mesh in memory: 967 of 90,041 in 111 of 144 buildings before, 0 of 89,173 after (ground tiles 0 before and after). Cause of the rejections: points along one straight wall are not exactly in line once computed, so the roof triangulation made triangles of no width lying on the wall, each a third face on the wall's top edge; `build_surroundings.py` now drops them, makes the roof use every piece of the outline as an edge, and checks that each building is a single skin open only at its foot. One file was built and inspected before any batch; then all 181 assets were built with no crash. Records: `docs/unreal/1529_wieland/review/phase4/append_check_buildings.json` and `build_records.jsonl`; journal lines K2 and K3 of `phase4_results.md`.
- 2026-11-11 16:45 and 16:50, two non-fatal "ensure" records from the same editor run (`..._0000`: no realtime override found in `EditorViewportClient.cpp` line 748, called from Python; `..._0001`: GPUScene transform mismatch, `GPUScene.cpp` line 392). An ensure is a warning that is reported and then continues; the editor kept running past both. Status: noted; recur only if the same Python viewport calls are repeated.
- 2026-11-11, the owner reported visual artefacts on the screen that remain after the Epic launcher and editor are closed, and suspected the GPU. Checked read-only: the System log shows no display-driver reset (Display 4101), `nvlddmkm` error or WHEA hardware error for three days; `C:\Windows\LiveKernelReports` (kernel GPU dumps) could not be read without administrator rights, so kernel reports are unchecked; GPU at 50 C idle; driver 610.88. Found: ZOTAC FireStorm 4.0 (`C:\Program Files (x86)\ZotacFireStorm\FireStorm_DID2704.cfg`) has `AutoOverClock=1` and `AutoRun=1`, and applies its `Start_OC` profile at every Windows start: core +100 MHz, memory +1000 MHz, power 105%, temperature target 85. Live readings match it: memory clock 12,201 MHz against the card's rated maximum of 11,201 MHz; power limit 336 W against the default 320 W. MSI Afterburner is also installed and starts with Windows through a scheduled task, with a stock profile (no offsets); two tuning tools both starting at boot can override each other. Separately, Windows GPU timeout detection is set to `TdrDelay=100` (seconds) and `TdrLevel=3` in `HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\GraphicsDrivers`, last written 2026-09-22, before this project, so a hung GPU is waited on for 100 seconds instead of 2 before Windows resets it. Cause of the artefacts: not proven. Leading suspect: the +1000 MHz memory overclock under the sustained heavy video-memory load of Nanite, Lumen and large textures; artefacts that outlive the application and leave no driver-reset record fit memory errors on an overclocked card better than a driver crash. No crash record of this project is a GPU crash. Next step (owner's action; a system setting): set FireStorm to the default profile and switch off its auto-overclock at startup, keep only one tuning tool starting with Windows, restart, and confirm the memory clock reads no more than 11,201 MHz under load (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=clocks.mem --format=csv`). If artefacts continue at stock clocks, test the card and the DisplayPort cable (Dell AW2723DF at 2560 x 1440, 239 Hz). Discriminating checks: a screenshot taken while the artefacts show (artefact in the image means the card's framebuffer: card, memory or overclock; clean image means monitor or cable); whether a full power-off clears them; and the fan under load, because both tools hold a 30% fan value and the card read 30% at idle (watch `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=temperature.gpu,fan.speed,power.draw,clocks.mem --format=csv -l 2` while the editor renders). The memory read 12,201 MHz at idle, so at 239 Hz the overclocked memory drives the desktop all the time, which is how artefacts can outlive the editor. Status: open, waiting on the owner.
- 2026-11-11 19:30, my error: a process listing printed the editor's full command line, which carries the Epic launcher's one-time `-AUTH_PASSWORD` exchange code, into the session transcript. It is a single-use login exchange code, very likely already spent by the editor at start-up. Prevention: never print `CommandLine` of processes started by a launcher; select process ID, start time and memory only, and mask `AUTH_PASSWORD=` in any helper output (the watcher's `digest.py` does). Status: resolved; owner told.
- 2026-11-11 19:45, automation set up at the owner's request: a watcher (`E:\Temp\claude\3d9c7db16e141ffd\e9961f27-3017-4918-b5e2-ab932667ff64\scratchpad\watch\watcher.ps1`) checks the Unreal agent every 15 minutes with plain rules (editor alive, crash record, chat ended, 45-minute stall) and asks Haiku only whether a still-working agent needs help; it relaunches a crashed editor itself, at most 3 times without a new commit, and wakes the supervising session for anything else. Computer use was not available to the supervising session, so the panel window cannot be typed into: follow-on phases run as headless Fable sessions (`run_worker.py`, same project folder, same agent server, `bypassPermissions` as the panel uses) in a terminal tab. Two findings while building it: `claude --bare` cannot be used under a subscription sign-in (it reads only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`); and in Windows PowerShell 5.1 an empty-string argument such as `--tools ""` is dropped when passed to a program, so the Haiku call is made from Python. Status: running.
- 2026-11-11 19:48-20:00, three faults while arming the watcher. (1) The watcher relaunched the editor at 19:48 while the owner was reopening it; the owner's copy briefly held port 8000 and closed, so the remaining copy started with "HttpListener unable to bind to 127.0.0.1:8000" and no agent server. Fix: closed it and relaunched once; the watcher now waits 5 minutes and rechecks before relaunching, and judges the server by an HTTP answer (`Get-NetTCPConnection` did not list the editor's listener at all). (2) `claude` 2.1.293 warns `unrecognized_model` for `claude-fable-5-2` and disables its advisor, but runs. (3) At 20:00 the progress display `run_worker.py` died with `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec can't encode character '→'` (Windows console code page); the agent process (Phase 4 session `0ce7509a-5e39-4bc3-9bd0-9ae6ff658f8a`) kept running and writing its record. Fix: `sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")`, display errors caught, lock holds the agent's own PID, and a resume argument added. Prevention: any Python that prints agent text on Windows must set UTF-8 output. Status: resolved; a stale `worker.lock` may remain after this orphaned run ends and must be removed before the next run.

### Phase 4 resumed and finished -- 2026-11-11 (headless session against the editor started at 19:52)

Entries from the session that resumed Phase 4. Results are in `docs/unreal/1529_wieland/phase4_results.md`.

- 2026-11-11 20:02, a static mesh asset made by Geometry Script with `GeometryScriptCreateNewStaticMeshAssetOptions.enable_nanite = True` had no Nanite data in 5.9.0. Found by inspecting the first asset before any batch. Fix: also set `options.nanite_settings` to a `MeshNaniteSettings` with `enabled = True`, and after creation read `StaticMeshEditorSubsystem.get_nanite_settings(mesh).enabled` and switch it on with `set_nanite_settings(mesh, settings, True)` if it is off (`_ensure_nanite` in `wieland_surroundings.py`). Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11 20:10, three neighbours' county outlines (which are roof outlines) reached 2 to 40 cm onto the lot, against the plat of survey. Found by `tools/measure_surroundings.py` after the build. Fix: `build_surroundings.py` cuts a neighbour back to the lot line, and numbers 1525 and the rear building back to their surveyed lines; the three assets were replaced in place with the new function `rebuild` (same asset, same object, new geometry and pivot). Prevention: measure against the survey before saving a batch as final. Status: resolved; number 1533's front corner still differs from the plat by 63.3 cm and needs the owner's tape (gap 18 in `site_gaps.md`).
- 2026-11-11, a false alarm: the first placement check compared an object's bounds with its file's bounds and read differences of metres, because files hold vertices that no triangle uses. Fix: the check compares only vertices that triangles use. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11 20:16, `World.streaming_levels` cannot be read from Python in 5.9.0 ("property not found"). Fix: a level that shows other levels by reference is put together with `unreal.EditorLevelUtils.add_level_to_world(world, path, unreal.LevelStreamingAlwaysLoaded)` and checked with `EditorLevelUtils.get_levels(world)`. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11 20:16, the Revit model's own site slab (`Site_GRADE_GRADE`, top at height 0) covers the new street, up to 92 cm above the new ground, and its massing of the neighbours (`Generic_Models_SURROUNDING_BUILDINGS`) stands inside the new neighbours. Both are Datasmith's and may not be edited. Workaround: `actor.set_is_temporarily_hidden_in_editor(True)` for renders and profiles; this is not saved and is lost when the level is loaded again, so it must be repeated. The lasting answer is the owner's (hide the massing; cut the slab back to the lot in Revit). A line trace from a station to find the ground hits the hidden slab unless hidden objects are ignored. Status: open, waiting on the owner.
- 2026-11-11 20:17, the first grey station image was flat and nearly white. Two causes. (1) The viewport of the new level was in the view mode Unlit; the console command `viewmode lightingonly` sent through `execute_console_command` has no effect on the editor's viewport. Fix: `unreal.AutomationLibrary.set_editor_viewport_view_mode(unreal.ViewModeIndex.VMI_LIGHTING_ONLY)` and read it back with `get_editor_active_viewport_view_mode()`; set it back to `VMI_LIT` afterwards. (2) A new directional light of 100,000 lux overexposes the editor's viewport, which has no exposure setting of its own in a new level. Fix: the placeholder sun is 10 lux; real exposure is Phase 8's. Prevention: read the view mode before every capture; a new level does not start in Lit. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11 20:22, journal times written from memory were wrong by up to 11 minutes (station images recorded as 20:24 to 20:30, taken at 20:17 to 20:19 by the files' own times). Fix: corrected in `phase4_results.md` from file times. Prevention: take every journal time from `date +%H:%M` or from the file written, never from an estimate. Status: corrected.
- 2026-11-11 20:23, a journal sentence was cut in the middle because its text, passed to a shell script in double quotes, held backticks and a backslash before a quote. Fix: mended by a script file. Prevention: journal text that holds backticks or backslashes goes through a script file or a quoted heredoc, not through a double-quoted shell argument; the same care for Python text that holds Windows paths (`\a`, `\b` and `\2` in ordinary strings turned three paths in the journal into nonsense, since mended). Status: corrected.
- 2026-11-11, compiled Python files (`tools/__pycache__/`) were committed once by `git add tools` and taken out again in commit `ef5ab64e`. Prevention: add files by explicit path; run tools with `python -B`. The folder is still untracked and not ignored. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11 20:23, profile M2 was taken with the editor's window not in the foreground (the session is unattended), with a different lighting set-up from M1, and without the viewport's pixel size on record. Its numbers (6.16 ms mean frame, 5.07 ms graphics card, 5,177 MB video memory) are therefore a reading of the new surroundings, not a like-for-like successor of M1 (9.07 ms). Prevention: `wieland_profile.start` should record the viewport's pixel size itself, and a milestone profile should be taken with the editor in the foreground. Status: recorded; Phase 5 can repeat the profile first.
- 2026-11-11, the advisor checkpoints of the owner's global instructions could not be made: this session has no advisor tool (the launcher reports it disabled for this model, as noted above). The closing diff was checked by the session itself against the scope instead. Status: noted for the owner.

### Phase 5 materials -- 2026-11-11 (headless session against the editor started at 19:52)

Entries from the session that carried out Phase 5, 20:43 to 22:05. Results are in `docs/unreal/1529_wieland/phase5_results.md`. The editor did not crash in this phase; free commit memory stayed between 32.4 and 36.8 GB (`review/phase5/memory_log.csv`). Times are given where the journal holds one; the other entries carry the date only.

- 2026-11-11, the agent server's `execute_python` refuses a plain string as its script. Fix: pass `{"script": {"language": "Python", "content": "<code>"}}`. Variables do not last from one call to the next; imported modules do, so lasting state lives in a module (`wieland_candidates._STATE`). Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, a call that waited on a long step (shader compiling, a batch of swatch pictures) timed out, and `time.sleep` is rejected inside the editor's Python. Fix: start the step, then poll in calls of 20 seconds or less with `await asyncio.sleep`. A refusal that names a "slow task" means the editor is busy; the same call is simply sent again. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, Windows paths written into Python text inside a Bash heredoc lost or changed their backslashes (once a control character, 0x82, in the journal's path `profiles\2026-11-11_M3\`; twice a `SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence`, one of them a `"\;"` written into `tools/phase5_licences.py`; once a raw string ending in a backslash, which is a syntax error). Fix: each mended (the journal by a script and rescanned for control characters, none left; the tool to `\\;` and rerun clean; the raw string replaced by `os.path.join`). Prevention: no Windows path or backslash in inline heredoc Python; use the Edit or Write tool, a script file, or `chr(92)`. The older defect of the same kind in `phase4_results.md` line 20 was left for the owner. Status: resolved, one older defect open.
- 2026-11-11, appending to the journal by heredoc failed on its backticks. Fix: the Edit and Write tools. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, the first commit of the phase lacked the `Co-Authored-By` line. Fix: every later commit carries it; the first was not rewritten. Status: noted.
- 2026-11-11, Git Bash turned an asset path beginning `/Game/` into a Windows path when it was passed as an argument. Fix: `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` before the command. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, scoring defect in `tools/phase5_score.py`: Autodesk's category "Woods___Plastics" was read as plastic, so wood materials were scored as plastics. Fix: the category is matched whole; the audit was scored again. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, the first swatch pictures had a colour cast from the stage and were cropped wrongly. Fix: neutral stage and a fixed crop in `tools/wieland_materials.py`; all pictures retaken. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, after the pictures of the Revit materials were taken, the editor marked 80 of the 82 Datasmith material packages as changed although nothing had been edited (rendering a material on a new mesh is enough). Fix: the packages were put back to their state on disk with `reload_dirty`; nothing of Datasmith's was saved. Prevention: read `dirty_packages()` after every capture run and never use "Save All". Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, the editor also marks the new candidates as changed after they are rendered. Putting them back to disk by reload made the second swatch run draw the image-based candidates white, and one candidate (`Travertine`) was afterwards found saved with every value cleared. Found by its swatch and confirmed by the new `verify_candidates()` (only that row differed). Fix: candidates marked as changed are saved by name, not reloaded; `Travertine` was rebuilt and saved; the station renders that had shown it plain (journal lines L11 and L12, one mesh slot of casework) were retaken in L14. Profile M3 "alternatives on" was not repeated: one slot of 3,106. Prevention: run `verify_candidates()` after any rebuild or reload and before any render that is kept. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, the station renders marked the level `L_Site_M2` as changed (temporary cameras, hidden slab). Fix: the level was opened again with `load_level(path, LevelEditorUnsavedChangesPolicy.DISCARD)`; it was never saved. A first attempt used a wrong level path (`/Maps/`). Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, `python -I` cannot import PIL on this machine (the isolated mode drops the user's site packages). Fix for image work on downloaded files: `python -P -B` with the script kept outside the data folder, which still keeps the data folder off the import path. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, ambientCG set `Bricks085` turned out unsuitable once unpacked (painted brick). Fix: `Bricks086` used in its place; `Bricks085` stays on disk unused and is listed for the owner. The downloads began some minutes before their journal line was written; the line was written and committed before any import. Status: resolved; the order of journal and download is noted as a lapse.
- 2026-11-11, printing a dictionary of textures as a call's result flooded the session's context. Prevention: return counts and names only. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, first pass of the candidate recipes: several colours were clamped, three asset names were guessed and wrong, Revit's own relief images were far too strong at full strength, and the fluted concrete-block image did not fit a plain block. Fix: colours converted from Revit's screen values without clamping; names read from the asset registry; NormalStrength 0.25 for Revit reliefs and the limestone relief left out; three images for plain block computed by `tools/phase5_make_textures.py`. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, milky and opal glass came out dark on the see-through parent. Fix: the light fittings' glass is an opaque white candidate; the Kohler frosted glass stays see-through and reads darker than it should; `Difused_glass` is glass at an opacity of 0.8. Status: workaround; listed for the owner.
- 2026-11-11, journal line L4 held an estimate of duration ("28 minutes") instead of a reading. Fix: corrected from the clock. Status: corrected.
- 2026-11-11, the first version of the seam measure in `tools/phase5_material_tests.py` compared the wrong pair of lines and failed seamless images. Fix: the step across the wrapping edge is compared with the strongest step inside the image. Status: resolved.
- 2026-11-11, the test table's first run passed 98 of 115 candidates. Fix: 15 recipes tuned in `tools/phase5_candidates.py` (`tune`), rebuilt and verified; 107 pass. The 8 that do not are recorded with reasons (4 smooth finishes at the threshold, 2 reeded glasses, 2 `Barham & Sons` floors on Revit's coarse image). Status: closed with reasons; the floors wait on the owner.
- 2026-11-11, video memory at its peak read 658 MB more in profile M3 than in the repeat of M2, in both M3 recordings alike, so it is what the editor had loaded by then (swatch level, 42 new images), not the alternatives. Status: recorded.
- 2026-11-11, found in the audit and not mended: the stair stringer meshes carry no texture coordinates; Substrate is switched on in the project; `/Game/Wieland1529/Scratch/M_Probe5` has been unsaved since Phase 1. Status: listed for the owner.
- 2026-11-11, steps 5.3 and 5.9 and the colour line could not be done as written: there are no photographs. Nothing was taken from Google Street View or map imagery. Status: open until the site visit.
- 2026-11-11, the advisor checkpoints could again not be made: this session has no advisor tool. The closing diff was checked by the session itself against the scope. Status: noted for the owner.

### Phase 6 paused by the owner -- 2026-11-11 22:48 (supervising session)

- Not an error: the owner asked to stop the unattended Phase 6 run immediately. The headless agent (session `85926560-462e-4bf5-9139-9a4483cae6e0`) was killed with `taskkill /T /F` mid-step J2 (step 6.2, north triplex kitchen). The 15-minute watcher was stopped first, so nothing will relaunch or restart the editor.
- State: J1 (step 6.1) done and committed in `6b98c89c`. J2 left no saved asset (no `Content\Wieland1529\Interiors\` folder on disk); unsaved meshes may sit in the editor's memory and should be discarded, not saved. The agent's partial `tools\wieland_refine.py` is committed as found.
- Resume: redo J2 from its start; see the status lines at the top of `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase6_results.md`. Re-arm `watcher.ps1` only when an unattended run is wanted again.
- Lesson: killing a worker mid-step costs at most one step because the journal commits a PENDING line before each step; prefer stopping at a journal commit when the owner allows the wait.

### Overnight idle of Phase 6 -- 2026-11-12 (supervising session)

- Error: the Phase 6 agent hit its usage limit at 00:24 ("resets 2:30am"). The watcher saw it at 00:41, escalated and exited, as it was built to. The supervising session was itself out of usage and could not act, so nothing ran from 02:30 to 09:07. The owner's instruction was that the work must not stop.
- Cause: the design depended on the supervisor answering every escalation; the watcher could restart the editor but not the agent, and it stopped watching after any escalation.
- Fix (09:11): `watcher.ps1` now runs detached (started through WMI, single copy via `watcher.pid`) and acts by itself: relaunches the agent when it has stopped mid-phase (a `| PENDING |` journal cell), stops a stuck agent after a usage limit and relaunches 5 minutes after the reset time parsed by `digest.py`, kills and relaunches after 90 minutes of no activity, and starts the next phase from `prompt_phase<N+1>.txt` when a phase closes. Prompts for Phases 7 to 11 were written ahead (commit `48f8441a`). A cap of 5 relaunches without a new commit stops loops. Anything it cannot handle goes to `alerts.txt`; it no longer exits.
- Verified: reset-time parsing against the real message (02:30 next day) and a dated form; both scripts parse; the detached launch path runs `run_worker.py` (it refused correctly while the lock was held). Not yet observed end to end on a real limit.
- Prevention: an unattended loop must never rely on the supervisor being available to continue.

- Not an error: the owner asked to stop the agent at a good spot. The watcher (pid 64020) was stopped first at 10:22 so it could not relaunch anything; `stop_at_commit.ps1` then waited for the agent's next journal commit and killed the headless agent (pid 56412) at 10:45 right after `c36d01ac` (J8 done: materials on the 92 uncovered Phase 4 buildings, saved by name).
- State: J1 to J8 done; no step in flight, nothing unsaved. Remaining: 7.1-b audit fixes (A5, ML2, A10), lawn (PENDING in J7, no licensed grass image), 7.3 vegetation, review renders. Phase 6 remains paused after J11; queue `6 8 9 10 11` is unchanged.
- Resume: re-arm `watcher.ps1` (detached); `current_phase.txt` is 7, so it relaunches Phase 7 from the journal. A stale `worker.lock` is cleared by the watcher.

### Phase 7: site, surroundings and vegetation -- 2026-11-12

Journal: `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase7_results.md` (J1 to J12). Every error of the phase, with cause, fix and status:

- The terminal's own connection to the editor's agent server (`unreal-mcp`) was refused at start (ECONNREFUSED): the editor was still loading. Fix: `tools\ue_call.py` speaks to the same server over HTTP. Status: workaround, used all phase.
- Wrong toolset name on the first call. Fix: the Python toolset is `editor_toolset.toolsets.python_scripting.PythonScriptingTools`, tool `execute_python`. Status: resolved.
- A Windows path with backslashes inside inline Python in a shell heredoc was mangled. Fix: forward slashes in inline Python; multi-line files through the Write or Edit tool. Status: resolved, then repeated in a new form (see the escape-sequence entry below).
- Journal times were first written from estimates. Fix: every time from `date +%H:%M`. Status: resolved; two later entries were one or two minutes ahead of the clock and were corrected.
- `try: import ...` in a one-line call failed (a one-line call cannot hold a block). Fix: plain import. Status: resolved.
- `unreal.GeometryScript_MeshBasicEdit` does not exist in 5.9; the functions are in `GeometryScript_MeshEdits`. Likewise `create_new_static_mesh_asset_from_mesh` is in `GeometryScript_NewAssetUtils`, not `GeometryScript_AssetUtils`. Fix: names read from `dir()` before use. Status: resolved.
- The mesh queries return their list and a flag as the last two values; the first unpacking was wrong. Fix: take `[-2:]`. Status: resolved.
- Console settings were not put back for three minutes: the picture helper kept the values to restore in module memory, which a reload wipes. Fix: the values are kept in a file (`review\phase7\scratch\settings_before.json`). Status: resolved.
- Free commit memory was 20 to 23 GB all phase, under the 30 GB rule, with nothing left to unload. Fix: none possible; every heavy step ran with the memory log on (`review\phase7\memory_log.csv`); the editor held 17 to 25 GB. Status: open, listed for the owner.
- `json` is not in scope in a `ue_call` expression. Fix: `ws.json`. Status: resolved.
- `get_num_triangles(0)` on a Nanite mesh returns the fallback count, not the real one. Fix: count from the dynamic mesh. Status: resolved.
- The first fit switched Nanite on for the 27 glass meshes (Nanite cannot show see-through materials). Fix: each mesh keeps the earlier mesh's setting. Side effect: four earlier glass materials were marked as changed in memory; they were never saved and the mark went with the editor restart at 10:31. Status: resolved.
- `SceneComponent` has no `bounds` in Python. Fix: `get_actor_bounds`. Status: resolved.
- A heredoc holding apostrophes broke the shell command twice (once at 09:xx, once in J9). Fix: Write or Edit tool for such text. Status: resolved; prevention: no apostrophes in heredocs.
- The earlier materials' folder was guessed wrong; `blueprint_get_size_x` was called on a material instance; a level path was compared with its object path. Fix: paths read from the asset registry, sizes from the texture, `get_outermost().get_name()`. Status: resolved.
- A texture's fourth channel needs an append node before a three-channel mix. Fix: `AppendVector`. Status: resolved.
- Poly Haven concrete and asphalt are warmer than Chicago's; Fix: grey controls in the ground material. Status: resolved (colour not verified: no photograph).
- Material instances are marked as changed again after first use. Fix: saved by name a second time. Status: resolved.
- The image library wiped the colour channels where the fourth channel was zero when resizing the ground-cover picture (dark outlines, car parks as concrete). Fix: each channel resized on its own, the fourth stored inverted. Status: resolved.
- `set_material` on the lot placeholder did not mark the level as changed. Fix: saved by name anyway. Status: resolved.
- `rev` does not exist in Git Bash. Fix: Python. Status: resolved.
- `StaticMesh` has no `get_num_uv_channels`. Fix: call removed. Status: resolved.
- First picture of a new material showed the engine's grid material: shaders were still compiling. Fix: wait and take a throw-away picture first. Status: resolved; it recurred once for the lawn (next entry but one).
- The Phase 4 building trial showed a maze pattern (the striped roofing image read a second time turned a quarter), then no windows (the limit used the lowest roof height of the lumpy laser-scan shape), then roofs white as snow (the aerial photograph is sunlit, not a colour measure). Fixes: second reading only shifted; typical roof height less 0.3 m; roof colour scaled so that the photograph's roadway reads as asphalt (factor 0.246). Status: resolved; the roofs still read lighter than the earlier buildings' when looking west.
- CRASH at 10:30: "Assertion failed: Index >= 0" in `UObjectArray.h` inside the Variant Manager's `SwitchOn`, when the list `Surroundings` was switched by script in an editor run in which the parent material had been rebuilt and temporary cameras stood in the level. Unsaved overrides on 144 buildings were lost (the assets were saved). Fix: editor restarted, overrides redone and saved at once; pictures set visibility directly and do not use the switch. The switch works in a fresh editor run (tested both ways, also for the new list `Season`). Status: workaround; cause not narrowed further. Prevention: save before any variant switch by script.
- A call printed 180 asset paths twice into the session. Fix: return counts only. Status: resolved (same lesson as 2026-11-11).
- Pictures from above were over-exposed (automatic exposure, dark sky in half the frame). Fix: steeper views without sky. Status: workaround; lighting is Phase 8.
- J9: the first backing wall of the 1525 house faced inward and could not be seen. Fix: rebuilt with its faces turned outward (`flip_normals`). Status: resolved.
- J9: the first ML2 test pictures showed the wrong building (the yellow-brick one north of 1533); the blank wall of 1533 faces the lot and stands behind the new house. Fix: the building level is switched to not visible for the test pictures only. That marked `L_Building` as changed without a change; it was not saved and the mark went when the level was opened again. Status: resolved.
- J9: brightness variation 0.18 made the new brick blotchy. Fix: 0.07 at 6 m. Status: resolved.
- J10: after a change to `wieland_ground.py` the rebuilt material was the old one: `ue_call` reloads only the modules named by its mode, and `import wieland_ground` inside another mode returns the copy already in memory. Fix: rebuild through the module's own mode (or `importlib.reload`) and read a value back from the instance before taking pictures. Status: resolved. Prevention: read back, do not trust "saved: true".
- J10: the lawn showed a blue sheen and wood chips close up. Fix: roughness never under 0.9, image at 0.6 m, dark end lifted, darker tints. Status: stand-in; passes from eye height at 2 m and more, fails closer than 1 m; owner decision.
- J11: the ground under 8 trees was not found: the ray stopped at buildings and at the hidden Revit objects, which still have collision. Fix: whatever is not site ground is left out and the ray is cast again. Status: resolved.
- J11: the first tree was 0.9 m too narrow: the models' widths were taken from the placed, turned trees of the earlier level. Fix: each model's own size is read in the editor (`model_sizes.json`). Status: resolved.
- J11: 17 of the 34 data points lie on a roadway or under a building: the laser scan gives the crown top, not the trunk. Fix: 14 trunks moved to the nearest free ground (0.6 to 3.6 m, recorded per tree); 3 left and flagged. Status: workaround until the site visit.
- J11: two of the profile recordings ran with the editor in the background (another window took the foreground between calls). Fix: the compared recordings were taken inside one call that brings the editor to the front every 10 seconds, in the order shown, hidden, shown. Status: resolved; the background recordings are kept and marked as not used.
- J11 and earlier: text for the journal written through a shell heredoc into ordinary Python strings turned `\v`, `\a` and `\202` in Windows paths into control characters (six places in `phase7_results.md`). Fix: found by scanning for control characters, repaired, and the closing text was written from a script file with raw strings. Status: resolved. Prevention: raw strings (`r"..."`) for any text with backslashes, and a scan for control characters before a commit.
- J12: one of 30 station pictures was missing after the first pass (the file did not appear within the wait). Fix: retaken. Status: resolved.
- The advisor checkpoints of the global rules could not be made: no advisor tool in this session. The plans and the closing diff were checked by the session itself. Status: noted for the owner.
- Not done, by scope or for lack of evidence: street furniture and fences (audit A8), planting, `L_Site_M4`. Status: listed for the owner in the journal.

### Phase 6 finish -- 2026-11-12

Journal: `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase6_results.md`, lines J13 to J15. Every error of the session, with cause, fix and status:

- Phase 7 left the Phase 6 top level `L_Site_M4` behind: it loaded none of the four Phase 7 levels (52 neighbour buildings missing, no new ground, no trees) and its shell level, a copy of `L_Building` from before Phase 7, still showed the Revit site slab and neighbour massing. Cause: Phase 7 worked on `L_Site_M2` only and listed `L_Site_M4` as an open item. Fix: the four levels added to `L_Site_M4` as always loaded; the two Revit objects made not visible by an override on the placed copies in `L_W1529_Shell`; record `review\phase6\finish\m4_fixes.csv`; both levels saved by name. Status: resolved. Prevention: a phase that changes a top level checks every other top level that loads the same sublevels.
- No picture could be taken for about ten minutes: `take_high_res_screenshot` and `HighResShot` returned without writing a file. Cause: the level viewport was not being drawn (a four-second probe recording read 0.11 ms of graphics-card time at 120 frames a second), most likely because another editor tab stood in front of it; the editor itself was responding and in the foreground. Fix: `EditorUtilitySubsystem.spawn_registered_tab_by_id("LevelEditorViewport")` brings the viewport tab forward (probe 12.2 ms, picture written). Status: resolved. Prevention: before pictures or a profile, run the short probe; a graphics-card time near zero means the viewport is hidden.
- The first grey picture of D3 was identical to the material picture. Cause: the console command `viewmode lightingonly` does not act on the editor viewport (already recorded on 2026-11-11 20:17 and not read before use). Fix: `AutomationLibrary.set_editor_viewport_view_mode` with a read-back before each picture. Status: resolved.
- The first tile pictures were blurred. Cause: the tile's 8,192-pixel images had not loaded at full size (texture streaming). Fix: `set_force_mip_levels_to_be_resident` on the three images for the time of the pictures; no asset changed. Status: resolved.
- One of the tile pictures (D4 whole view under the grazing lamp) was not written within the wait. Fix: none; the sheet uses the close views. Status: accepted.
- My first count of objects with a rounded mesh read 0, then 1,977, against 3,493 in the journal. Cause: a wrong folder in the search, then counting objects where the journal counts mesh components. Fix: counted per level by component: 427 + 1,151 + 504 + 1,086 + 325 = 3,493. Status: resolved.
- Switching the interior lists and showing single unit levels mark the unit levels as changed although their saved state is unchanged. Fix: `L_Site_M4` opened again from disk without saving after each such step. Status: workaround, as in Phase 6 before.
- Free commit memory was 20.5 GB at the start (under the 30 GB rule, nothing to unload) and rose above 30 GB once `L_Site_M2` was closed; it stayed above 30 GB during profile M4. Status: noted.
- Acceptance lines that cannot be met as written: no Revit views of D1 to D5 exist (line 1); sharp edges are not zero (line 3); the halves are not mirror images (line 4). Status: reported with numbers; listed for the owner.
- The advisor checkpoints of the global rules could not be made: no advisor tool in this session. Status: noted for the owner.

### Phase 8 paused by the owner -- 2026-11-12 17:09 (supervising session)

- Not an error: the owner asked to stop before usage ran out. The watcher (pid 61952) was stopped first, then the headless Phase 8 agent (pid 84332) and its children, with `taskkill /T /F`, two minutes after `0a834488` (J7 done).
- State: J1 to J4, J6 and J7 are done. J5 (fixture table, data-sheet search) was marked in progress, so inspect its outputs before redoing it. Phases 6 and 7 are DONE. The queue after Phase 8 is `9 10 11`.
- Resume: re-arm `watcher.ps1` (it relaunches Phase 8 from the journal), or paste `KICKOFF_PHASE8.md` into the editor panel with the monitor off.
- Resumed 19:22 by the supervisor: editor reopened (pid 68852), Phase 8 agent relaunched headless, watcher re-armed (pid 27852).
- Error found before resuming (a latent risk, not yet triggered): the watcher's "phase finished" test looked only for a `| PENDING |` cell. Phase 8's J5 reads "in progress" instead, so the watcher would have taken Phase 8 as done and started Phase 9. Fix: a phase now counts as finished only when its first `Status:` line says DONE. Verified: the script parses; Phases 6 and 7 read DONE, and Phase 8 reads PAUSED, so it stays open.

### Phase 8 paused again by the owner -- 2026-11-12 20:48 (supervising session)

- Error, unresolved: the editor's agent server (port 8000) stopped answering at 19:53, about 13 minutes after J9 (path-traced ground truth) began. It was still silent at 20:48, while Windows reported the editor (pid 68852) as responding. Free commit memory was 27.6 GB, below the 30 GB floor. Probable cause, unconfirmed: a long path-traced render was blocking the editor's game thread, which serves the agent server. Memory pressure is a possible factor.
- Action: on the owner's request the watcher (pid 27852) and the headless agent (pid 85404) were stopped. The editor was left open and was not saved.
- Next step: before any resume, check that the agent server answers. If it does not, restart the editor (Don't Save) and redo J9 at a smaller sample count or resolution, checking free commit memory first. The watcher only alerted on SERVER_DOWN and never stepped in. Consider having it treat more than 30 minutes of silence from the agent server during a PENDING step as a stall.
- Update 22:35, cause now better supported (still not proven): the renders kept going while the port was silent. `review\phase8\ground_truth\` holds full-size path-traced pictures of S1 (19:46), D3 (20:08), S2 to S6 and S9 (20:11 to 20:24), D1 (20:27) and D2 (20:39). `runs.jsonl` gives 134 to 662 seconds per picture at 1024 samples. D4 and D5 were not written; `job.json` (20:51) names D5. So the silence was the editor busy rendering, not a dead server, and the watcher's SERVER_DOWN alert was a false alarm during renders. Memory was not the cause of a crash: there was none.
- Resolved for the resume: the owner closed the editor without saving; it was reopened at 22:31 (pid 61556) with 52.5 GB free commit. The Phase 8 status line tells the agent to keep the eleven finished pictures, render only D4 and D5, and finish J9.
- Prevention: during a path-traced step, expect the agent server to be silent for up to about 11 minutes per 4K picture; check the output folder before treating it as down.

### Phase 8 J9: GPU crash on the first path-traced frame after the restart -- 2026-11-12 22:36 (supervising session)

- Error: the editor (pid 61556, opened 22:31) stopped with a GPU crash at 22:36: `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG` (device removed). The owner saw the "GPU crashed" dialog. Log: `D:\Unreal_Engine_Projects\Wieland1527 5.9\Saved\Logs\Wieland1527-backup-2026.11.13-04.39.07.log`, lines 3343 to 3774; Aftermath dump `D3D12.0.2026.11.12-22.36.29.nv-gpudmp` in the same folder.
- What was running: a Movie Render Graph path-traced trial at 1280 by 720, warm-up frame 1 of 64, with r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode 2. The active GPU step was `NaniteRayTracing::UpdateBLAS > HierarchyTraversal` (building the ray-tracing copy of the Nanite meshes), before `PathTracing` started. The GPU timed out after 5 seconds and was removed at 22:36:29. Video memory used was 7.95 GB of a 15.3 GB budget, so this was not running out of video memory.
- Probable cause, unconfirmed: in a freshly opened editor, the first path-traced frame builds the Nanite ray-tracing structures for the whole loaded scene in one GPU job, and that job ran past the Windows GPU timeout. The same renders worked earlier in a long-running editor where those structures already existed. Possible contributors: the GPU overclock (owner item "FireStorm overclock") and the Windows GPU timeout at its 2-second default (no TdrDelay value is set). Neither may be changed without the owner's approval.
- State: an editor was running again at 22:39 (pid 40852) and the headless agent (pid 82976) was still active. The agent's crash rule applies: if the same step crashes twice, change one thing at a time, for example let the scene settle in the real-time view before rendering, or render with r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode 0.
- Owner, 2026-11-12 after the crash: the FireStorm overclock recommendation had already been applied before this crash, so the GPU overclock is ruled out as a cause. The owner asked for the Windows GPU timeout to be raised; the supervising session gave the owner the `reg add` commands for `TdrDelay` and `TdrDdiDelay` = 60 (needs an administrator shell and a Windows restart), and did not change the setting itself.
- 22:43: the owner set TdrDelay and TdrDdiDelay to 60 and restarted Windows (read back as 60 at 22:53). The restart stopped the editor and the agent before the retry rendered anything. The agent's own journal entry adds that the log shows a failed shader check in `NaniteClusterRayTracing.usf` line 539, so this may be an engine fault in the Nanite ray-tracing build rather than a slow GPU task; the longer time limit may not prevent it. Resumed 22:55 with the agent's plan: let the level settle five minutes, then render D4 and D5 one at a time.
- Status: open. Update this entry with the outcome of the agent's retry.
- Outcome (Phase 8 agent, 2026-11-13): the retry worked. After a five minute settle D4 and D5 were path traced at full size without a crash (editor pid 12364). The same shader check failed again at 00:24 on 2026-11-13, this time in real time with `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode 2` and with TdrDelay 60 in force, so the longer time limit does not prevent it. Status: not fixed (engine fault); avoided by keeping `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` at 0 in real time and by letting the level settle before a path-traced render. See the Phase 8 section below.

### Phase 8: lighting and atmosphere -- 2026-11-12 16:38 to 2026-11-13 (Phase 8 agent; journal `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase8_results.md`)

Every error of the phase, with cause, fix and status. "J" numbers are the journal's lines.

- J3, sun object: the Sun Position plugin's sun object keeps the year 2019 whatever is set. Cause: a fixed value in the plugin's object. Fix: a plain directional light placed from the calculated bearing and height (`tools\wieland_lighting.py`). Status: fixed; 0.05 and 0.06 degree from NOAA.
- J3, brightness readings came back clamped at 1.0. Cause: the scene capture reads after tone mapping. Fix: read before tone mapping and multiply by 2^EV. Status: fixed.
- J3, readings of zero. Cause: the editor window was minimized, so nothing was drawn. Fix: `window_restore()` before every reading. Status: fixed.
- J3 and J4, first sun reading 58,872 lux on the card, under the plan's 80,000 to 100,000. Cause: the atmosphere takes away part of the sun's set value. Fix: sun set to 140,000 and then 160,000 lux; card reads 91,086 lux. Status: fixed.
- J3 and J4, sky share too low (7,512 lux) and fog too grey at density 0.02. Fix: haze 0.05, fog density 0.005; sky reads 17,985 lux. Status: fixed.
- J4, clouds make the sky reading drift by about 2,000 lux, and the cost of fog and clouds is inside the run-to-run spread. Status: noted; not measurable separately.
- J3, the sidewalk material reflects about 18 percent, less than real broom-finished concrete (25 to 35 percent). Status: listed for the owner (a material decision).
- J5, the first session left downloads but no fixture table. Cause: the owner's pause at 17:09. Fix: J5 continued from the downloads on resume. Status: fixed.
- J5, makers' photometric files not reachable by a script: XAL, Kohler and Alcon refuse (403), Kuzco refuses or not found (429, 404), Simes needs a login, Visual Comfort not found (404), the EAE archive is 149 MB (over the 50 MB approval). Fix: files delivered inside the Revit families where they exist, otherwise a generic light shape marked as a placeholder. Status: workaround; 9 kinds (339 lights) wait on the owner.
- J5, only 8 photometric files came with the Datasmith export, and no download carries a licence statement. Fix: files kept outside the repository (`E:\Wieland1529_site_data\photometry\`), listed in `asset_downloads.csv` with that note. Status: listed for the owner.
- J5 against J8, the count of fixture kinds is written as 21 and as 22. Cause: a counting slip in the J8 text. Fix: the table has 21 rows; the acceptance table says so. Status: fixed in the acceptance table.
- J6, my script used the unit name "CANDELA"; the engine's name is "CANDELAS". Fix: corrected. Status: fixed.
- J6, the engine uses a photometric file only as a shape and takes the brightness from lumens divided by 4 pi, which is wrong for a directed fixture. Fix: each light is set in candela with the file's peak value. Check: 733.44 lux against 740.2 calculated (0.9 percent low). Status: fixed.
- J8, the 571 imported lights were static and off, and pointed sideways. Fix: lit lights made movable and aimed (198 straight down), as overrides in the unit levels, never in a Datasmith asset. Status: fixed; 314 lit, 257 off.
- J8, two recessed fixtures have their two lights in different unit levels. Fix: the lone light stays off. Status: workaround.
- J8, 28 fixture bodies have no light in the export, and fixture lenses do not glow. Status: listed for the owner.
- J8, the 4th-floor exposure volume's edges were first found with a trace that hit fixtures. Fix: the trace skips fixtures. Status: fixed.
- J9, the agent server was silent from 19:53 to 20:48. Cause: the editor was busy path tracing (not a dead server). Fix: long background calls (`j9_long.py`); the output folder is checked before the server is called down. Status: fixed.
- J9, GPU crash at 22:36 (entry above). Status: avoided, see the outcome line above.
- J9, my call that started D4 had a 10 minute limit and was cut off while the render went on. Fix: the record was written by hand, the temporary camera and the Nanite setting were put back by the next call and read back. Status: fixed.
- J9, most tree leaves were missing from path-traced pictures. Cause: `r.RayTracing.Nanite.MaxAppendInstances` 10,000. Fix: 400,000 for the render only, put back afterwards. Status: fixed for renders; the project value is unchanged.
- J9, Quick Render ignores an exposure volume that has edges. Fix: the temporary camera carries the exposure. Status: workaround.
- J9, D2 (662 s) and D4 (about 1,125 s) are over the 10 minute budget and D4 is grainy. Status: listed for the owner.
- J9, the status notes said eleven finished pictures; there were ten. Status: corrected in the journal.
- J10 and J11, three views fail the tolerance (S9, D2, D3). Cause: the neighbours' houses are empty shells with see-through windows (S9); tinted window glass and smoke-glass pendant shades are treated differently by the two renderers (D2, D3). Fix: none inside lighting settings. Status: open; surroundings and material work, re-measured in Phase 12.
- J11, one trial (Nanite mode 2 at S1) ran before its PENDING line was written. Status: recorded in the J11 line.
- J11, ray-traced shadows trial: the editor fell under one frame a second for about ten minutes, its memory rose from 21.5 to 34.5 GB and free commit fell to 22 GB, under the 30 GB floor. Fix: `r.RayTracing.Shadows 0` set through the agent server while the render was waiting; all values read back as before. An autosave copy of `L_Site_M4` was written to `Saved\Autosaves` (the level file itself was not saved). Status: setting not usable.
- J11, Lumen World Cache could not be tried. Cause: `r.Lumen.WorldCache.Supported` is 0 and can only be changed in a project settings file. Status: not done; listed for the owner.
- J12, GPU crash at 00:24:15 on 2026-11-13 during the profile variant with `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode 2`: `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG`, shader check `NaniteClusterRayTracing.usf:539`, log `D:\Unreal_Engine_Projects\Wieland1527 5.9\Saved\Logs\Wieland1527-backup-2026.11.13-06.24.24.log` line 14546, crash folder `Saved\Crashes\UECC-Windows-3EDDE25247B19DC7DE1E579788F47784_0001`, video memory 8,235 of 15,280 MB. Fix: the variant was not repeated; the watcher restarted the editor (pid 25884) and nothing was lost. Status: not fixed (engine fault); the setting is not recommended.
- J12, the profile's viewport is 2,038 by 1,198 pixels where M4's was 2,038 by 782, the editor was in the foreground for only part of some recordings, and one recording holds one false frame of 1.8 billion ms (its repeat is clean). Status: stated with the numbers; M6 is over the 16.67 ms limit on every path, which Phase 9 takes up.
- All of Phase 8: no advisor tool in this session. Status: noted for the owner.
- 23:01 to 23:30: the retry worked. With the level left to settle first, D4 and D5 rendered, and J10 and J11 finished.
- Second occurrence, 2026-11-13 00:24, during J12 (profile M6, which includes runs with `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` 2 and with hit lighting): `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG` with the same shader assertion, `NaniteClusterRayTracing.usf:539 - RequestData.BLASDataIndex != RequestData.BaseBLASDataIndex`, in `NaniteRayTracing::UpdateBLAS > HierarchyTraversal`. Video memory 8.2 of 15.3 GB. Log `Wieland1527-backup-2026.11.13-06.24.24.log`. The 60-second Windows time limit did not prevent it, which fits an engine fault rather than a slow GPU task. The editor restarted itself at 00:24:26 (pid 25884); the agent (pid 16036) carried on.
- Cause, now well supported: the Nanite ray-tracing build in UE 5.9 (switched on by `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` 2) can fail its own check and hang the graphics card. It happened on the first frame after a level opened, and again during real-time profiling.
- Prevention, added to the prompts of Phases 12, 9, 10 and 11: leave `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` at 0 except for path-traced renders. For those, let the level settle five minutes first and render one picture at a time. Never adopt mode 2 for the real-time walk-through; report it to the owner as unstable in 5.9.
- Status: workaround in place. The underlying engine fault is not fixable here; a future engine update may fix it.

### Supervisor made self-sustaining -- 2026-11-13 01:05 (supervising session)

- Owner request: "ensure the editor continues despite any restart or crashes, phase endings or anything".
- Gaps found in `watcher.ps1` (scratchpad `watch\` folder), none an error yet, all able to stop the build:
  - after 3 editor restarts, or 5 agent relaunches, with no new commit it stopped for good;
  - an editor that ran but was hung kept the agent server silent, and the watcher only alerted;
  - after a phase ended it waited up to 15 more minutes, because it took its own agent's last activity for an editor-panel agent;
  - it checked only every 15 minutes;
  - nothing restarted the watcher itself after it died or Windows restarted (the 22:43 restart stopped everything until the supervisor stepped in).
- Fix:
  - checks every 5 minutes, with the cheap model's judgement still about every 15 minutes;
  - after the caps it backs off instead of stopping: an editor restart every 30 minutes, an agent relaunch every 60 minutes;
  - an editor whose agent server is silent for 60 minutes while the agent is idle for 60 minutes is ended (nothing is saved) and restarted;
  - its own finished agent no longer counts as an editor-panel agent, so the next phase starts at the next check;
  - new `keepalive.ps1` restarts the watcher within 2 minutes. It is started at sign-in by `C:\Users\cowey\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\wieland_keepalive.cmd`, at the owner's request. A `STOP` file in the watch folder pauses it.
- Verified: both scripts parse; the watcher was ended on purpose at 01:02 and the keep-alive restarted it at 01:04; the Phase 12 agent kept running. The previous watcher is kept as `watcher.ps1.bak_1113`.
- Limit: after a Windows restart, Windows must be signed in for the Startup entry to run. Ending a hung editor discards unsaved changes; the agents save named assets before each commit.

- J5, the temporary camera of each render left the top level `L_Site_M4` marked unsaved. Fix: saved by name once the camera was gone (`review\phase12\scripts\save_site_level.py`). Status: fixed.
- J5, real-time against ground truth: 8 of 12 views pass (Phase 8: 9). D2 now passes; D4 (-30.8 percent) and D5 (-11.4 percent) now fail; S9 and D3 fail as before. Cause for D4 as read from the pictures, not isolated by a trial: the frosted lamp shade and the white finishes make the path-traced bathroom brighter while the real-time picture stays as it was. Status: open; a lighting matter for the owner and Phase 9.
- Whole phase, no advisor tool in the session. Fix: the closing check of the changes against the scope was done by hand. Status: reported to the owner.

### Phase 9: optimization -- 2026-11-13 03:22 to 07:12 (Phase 9 agent; journal `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase9_results.md`)

Errors and surprises of the phase, each with cause, fix and status. "Jn" is the journal line.

- J1 and J2, the end times first written in the journal (03:58 and 04:00) were mistyped. Cause: typed from memory instead of read from the clock. Fix: corrected in J2 (J1 ended 03:37, J2 03:56). Status: fixed.
- J2, view D2 read +12.6 to +14.3 percent against the ground truth with the scene exactly as Phase 12 left it, while Phase 12's own picture of 02:08 read +2.4 percent. Cause: not found. Ruled out by trials: MegaLights on or off, the lamps (hidden: same reading), the number of lit lamps (unchanged), ray-traced lamp shadows. Fix: none; from then on every picture check was made twice, against the ground truth and against a picture of the unchanged state taken at the same moment, so that the changes of this phase are judged on their own. Status: open, for the owner (see J8).
- J3, the session that began the phase ended in the middle of step 9.3 (context ran out). Cause: long unattended run. Fix: the next session resumed from the PENDING line at 05:15 and looked at the files before repeating anything. Status: handled.
- J3, the trial `T_vsm_bias05` of the first session is invalid. Cause: it was run at screen percentage 100 instead of the tier's 82, because the percentage was passed by hand with each call. Fix: `review\phase9\scripts\gpu_passes.py` now always adds the tier's screen percentage; the trial was repeated as `T_s_bias05`. Status: fixed; the invalid folder is kept and not used.
- J3, a figure of trial `T_s_bias05` was first written wrongly in the journal. Cause: read from the wrong station's row. Fix: corrected from the summary file. Status: fixed.
- J3, the shadow pass at D2 read 6.3 ms and looked like the place to save time, but no shadow setting changed it. Cause: the graphics card works two queues at once; the shadow pass was waiting while Lumen traced rays, so Lumen's cost showed under the shadow heading (a 12-byte buffer clear read 2.2 ms). Fix: no shadow change; the Lumen change of J4 took shadows at D2 from 6.34 to 1.11 ms. Status: explained. Lesson: a pass time in a parallel frame is not proof of that pass's own cost; change one thing and measure.
- J3 and later, journal text written through a shell here-document lost its backslashes (Windows paths came out without separators), and a `sed` command with backslashes in it failed and stopped the chain of commands after it. Cause: the shell eats backslashes in unquoted here-documents and in `sed` patterns. Fix: journal text with paths is written with the file-editing tool; the damaged lines were rewritten. Status: fixed; rule already in the plan, now followed.
- J3, `review\phase9\scripts\table.py` printed an empty table. Cause: it reads the analysis files, and `tools\analyze_profile_csv.py` had not been run on the new recording yet. Fix: run the analysis first. Status: fixed.
- J4, three recordings of state C2 are invalid (`C2_window_minimised`, `C2_window_minimised_2`, `C2_behind_browser`). Cause: from about 05:40 somebody was working in a web browser on the computer; the editor was minimized twice (a minimized editor draws nothing: 2.6 ms frames with 0.08 ms of graphics-card time) and then stood behind the browser. Fix: the tools now record for every recording the share of time the editor was in front and whether its window was minimized; a fourth recording was taken after the computer had been idle for two and a half minutes, and the final recording `M7` at 06:18 with the editor in front throughout. Status: handled; invalid folders kept as evidence.
- J4, the measuring tool brought the editor to the front every ten seconds by pressing the Alt key and switching windows, also while the person was typing. Cause: `to_front()` in `tools\wieland_profile.py` was written for an empty computer. Fix: `polite_front` in `tools\phase9_profile_m7.py` does it only when keyboard and mouse have been idle for 10 seconds, and never presses a key otherwise. Status: fixed; the earlier key presses between 05:40 and about 05:55 may have disturbed the person's work, reported to the owner.
- J4, the fourth C2 recording has three frames over 33.3 ms on the exterior path (37.0, 38.1, 60.2 ms). Cause: stalls of the game thread at the moment the person switched windows; the graphics card was steady. Fix: none needed; the clean recording `M7` (computer idle) has none. Status: explained.
- J8, free commit memory was 28.3 GB before the final pictures, under the 30 GB line. Cause: the editor itself held 25.4 GB and the person's programs the rest; the editor's garbage collection freed nothing and nothing of this work was left to unload. Fix (workaround): one picture first as a trial (free commit stayed at 25.6 GB or more), then the other eleven one at a time; lowest reading 25.2 GB, no crash. Status: handled.
- J8, real-time against ground truth: 9 of 12 views pass (Phase 12: 8). D4 and D5 now pass; D2 (+13.9 percent) now fails; S9 and D3 fail as before, D3 worse (+62.1 percent). Cause: every interior view is brighter than in Phase 12's pictures of 02:03 to 02:10; not caused by the two changes of this phase (same-moment pairs differ by at most 0.6 points); what changed between 02:10 and 03:50 was not found. Status: open, for the owner. Not tried: whether a restart of the editor brings the earlier readings back.
- J8, with MegaLights switched off again by console variable the interior pictures came out far too bright (D3 +251, D4 +222 percent), which they did not at 03:50. Cause: not found; the state "MegaLights off" is no longer reproducible in this editor session. Fix: none; the before-state B0 was measured at 03:30 when it was still good. Status: open; do not use the switched-off state as a reference without restarting the editor.
- J10, the hold at S1 of recording `M7` has one stall of 1.1 seconds. Cause: the editor's autosave, which writes a spare copy of every level marked unsaved every ten minutes; five levels are marked unsaved without any change of content (`L_Site_M4` from the temporary camera of the pictures, the four unit levels `L_W1529_*` from being shown and hidden for the recordings). Fix: none in the project (saving them would save levels this phase did not mean to change); the soak report counts the walks with an autosave apart. Status: explained; goes away when the editor is closed without saving those levels, and does not exist in a packaged build.
- J10, the render thread's time in the recordings (9.8 to 12.0 ms) looks over its budget of 8.0 ms. Cause: the figure includes 4.9 to 6.1 ms of waiting for the graphics card. Fix: the waiting column (`Exclusive/RenderThread/EventWait/Visibility`) is subtracted; its own work is 4.9 to 6.1 ms. Status: explained.
- Whole phase, the editor's viewport is 2,038 by 1,198 pixels and cannot show the plan's 2,560 by 1,440 picture on this monitor. Fix (workaround): measured at the same number of computed pixels (screen percentage 82), with the two full-picture passes multiplied by 1.51. Status: workaround; a measurement in a packaged build at the real size is still open (Phase 10).
- Whole phase, a foreground `sleep` chained with other commands is refused by the session's shell tool. Fix: wait with a loop on a condition, or in the background. Status: handled.
- Whole phase, no advisor tool in the session. Fix: the closing check of the changes against the scope was done by hand. Status: reported to the owner.
- J9, the two-hour soak was stopped after 42 of 120 walks (42 minutes). Cause: the owner's decision, recorded in the journal's status line at 07:06 by the session before, to start Phase 10 sooner; the session that closed the phase started at 07:06 with the stock kickoff text (which still said the owner was away and asked for the full soak) and acted on the journal's note, not on a word from the owner. Fix: stopped with `review\phase9\scripts\soak_stop.py`, every setting put back (`soak_end.json`). Status: acceptance line met in part (no crash, video memory growth 0.0 percent over 42 minutes); reported to the owner, who can have the full soak run again.
- J9, the soak shows 23 frames over 33.3 ms in 216,996. Cause: 14 are one stall each in the 14 walks in which the engine's own recorder (`CsvProfile`) was started and stopped to read the video memory; 8 are the editor's autosave in walk 5; 1 (37.0 ms, walk 9) falls at the instant the recorder for walk 10 started and is probably the same stall, not proven. Fix: none during the run; `soak_report.py` counts the three kinds apart. Status: explained but for the one frame. Lesson: a soak that must prove "no frame over 33.3 ms" should not start the engine's recorder inside the walks (read video memory between walks, or another way) and should run with autosave off.
- J9, the status note of 07:06 said 41 walks; 42 are in the log. Cause: the 42nd walk ended at 07:06:28, after the note was written and before the stop at 07:07:16. Fix: the journal counts 42. Status: fixed.
- Closing, the working copy still shows `profiles\2026-11-12_M6\memory_log_phase8.csv` as changed. Cause: the memory log process of Phase 8 (process 30032) is still running and appends to it. Fix: left alone (not this phase's process; it ends by itself); the Phase 9 log process was stopped at 07:08. Status: reported to the owner.

### Nanite ray-tracing crash, third occurrence -- 2026-11-13 08:01 (supervising session)

- Error: the editor hung the graphics card (`DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG`) at 08:01 during Phase 10 J7, a path-traced picture of S8. Same assertion as at 22:36 and 00:24: `NaniteClusterRayTracing.usf:539 - RequestData.BLASDataIndex != RequestData.BaseBLASDataIndex`, in `NaniteRayTracing::UpdateBLAS`. `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` had been set to 2 at 08:00, after the five-minute settle the earlier workaround required; video memory 8.2 of 15.3 GB.
- Cause: all three crashes happened with mode 2 on, none with it off. Neither the five-minute settle nor the 60-second Windows time limit prevented it. The mode 2 build is unreliable in UE 5.9 on this machine.
- Fix: mode 2 is now off for every render, path-traced included, in the prompts of Phases 10 and 11. Path-traced pictures from now on trace the meshes' simplified fallback copies, and the sheets say so. The supervisor stopped the Phase 10 agent at 08:05; the watcher restarts the editor and relaunches Phase 10, which re-renders S8 with mode 0. (While stopping it, a combined `taskkill` and `Remove-Item` command was refused by the shell's safety check; `Stop-Process` alone worked.)
- Status: workaround in place and proven once. Outcome of the S8 re-render (Phase 10 agent, 08:18): with mode 0 the path-traced plan picture rendered in 56 seconds without a fault after a settle of five minutes twenty-one seconds; the trees are drawn as coarse triangles (fallback copies), walls and roofs show no holes at that size. `tools\wieland_truth.py` no longer sets mode 2 by default.

### Phase 10: reality-match review -- 2026-11-13 07:14 to 08:23 (Phase 10 agent, two sessions; journal `docs\unreal\1529_wieland\phase10_results.md`)

Errors and surprises of the phase, each with cause, fix and status. "Jn" is the journal line.

- J1 and J9, after a restart the editor's agent server accepted connections but ran nothing (eleven minutes at 07:16; again at 08:11). Cause: a window "Restore Packages" (the editor's offer to bring back automatic saves of levels that were marked unsaved when it closed) waits for an answer and blocks every tool; the tools answer "the Unreal Editor is running a slow task". Fix: close that window without restoring (a close message sent to the window whose title is "Restore Packages"); the levels held no work. Status: handled both times. Lesson: after any crash or forced close, look for this window first.
- J1 and J9, the restarted editor opened `/Game/Wieland/R15_Landscape/L_R15_Production` as its start level. Cause: the project's start-level setting still names the earlier production level. Fix: open `/Game/Wieland1529/L_Site_M4` by script each time; nothing saved. Status: workaround; changing the setting is a change to a project setting file and was left for the owner.
- J9, for a few seconds after the restart the tool call failed with "Toolset 'editor_toolset.toolsets.python_scripting.PythonScriptingTools' not found". Cause: the editor's Python tools register a little after the agent server starts answering. Fix: wait and repeat. Status: handled.
- J1 to J3, times in the journal were typed from an estimate and were wrong by up to seven minutes. Cause: not read from the clock. Fix: `review\phase10\scripts\journal.py` writes every time from the clock. Status: fixed; the wrong times are corrected in the journal.
- J1, Phase 9's count of views that pass real-time against path-traced (9 of 12) does not hold. Cause: a state of Phase 9's long editor session made every interior brighter; after a fresh start the saved scene gives Phase 12's 8 of 12 (S9, D3, D4, D5 fail; D3, D4, D5 repeat within 1.5 percent). Fix: none needed in the scene; the review counts 8. Status: explained as a session state; what in the session caused it is not known. Lesson: judge real-time pictures in a freshly started editor.
- J1 to J4, free commit memory was 25.8 to 29.2 GB, under the 30 GB line, with nothing left to unload. Cause: other programs held about 33 GB. Fix (workaround): only light work was done in that state (real-time pictures one first, ray casts), with the memory log running. Status: handled; no crash from memory.
- J3, a plan picture with a parallel-projection camera came out black with a thin blue arc. Cause: the quick render does not draw the scene for such a camera. Fix: a camera 2 km above the lot with a 3.44 degree lens shows the same 120 m square (a 12 m roof at the edge is drawn 0.36 m further out than its foot). Status: workaround in use.
- J4, the survey tie at number 1525's north-east corner first read 240 cm. Cause: a one-storey rear part 2.4 m deep stands east of the main house and was taken for the wall. Fix: the reading was repeated counting only surfaces higher than 5 m: 10 cm. Status: fixed.
- J6, the sheets and the tolerance table first said no street furniture exists in the scene, but the pictures show stoops, railings, fences and a utility pole. Cause: they are part of the merged meshes of the earlier neighbourhood model that Phase 7 kept, not separate objects. Fix: the row was reworded and all sheets made again; verdict unchanged (could not be checked). Status: fixed.
- J7, 08:01, the graphics card hung (`DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG`) during a path-traced plan picture of S8 and the editor closed itself. Cause: `r.RayTracing.Nanite.Mode` 2, the engine check in `NaniteClusterRayTracing.usf` line 539, third occurrence; a five-minute settle did not prevent it. Fix: see J9. Status: fixed by never using mode 2.
- Between J7 and J9, the supervisor stopped the first session at 08:05 before its closing step J8. Fix: the second session resumed from the journal at 08:11, marked J8 as not carried out and moved the closing to J10. Status: handled.
- J9, the S8 path-traced picture was made with mode 0 at 08:18 (after a settle of five minutes twenty-one seconds, one picture, 2,160 by 2,160, 1,024 samples, 56 seconds). No crash. What mode 0 changes: the trees are drawn as coarse triangles (fallback copies of the meshes); walls and roofs show no holes at that size. Status: done. To keep it so, `tools\wieland_truth.py` now uses mode 0 for the Path Tracer unless a caller names another mode, and `review\phase10\scripts\s8_pt.py` refuses to render unless the variable reads 0.
- J9, free commit fell from 37.0 to 24.6 GB during the 56-second path-traced render (the editor went from 17 to 30 GB). Cause: the Path Tracer builds its ray-tracing copy of the whole scene. Fix: none needed; it started above the 30 GB line and came back to 33.6 GB. Status: recorded. Lesson: a path-traced picture of this scene needs about 13 GB above the editor's resting commit.
- J9, the real-time plan picture of S8 is 38.5 percent brighter than the path-traced one and fails. Cause (seen, not proven by a trial): from 2 km up the real-time renderer draws far, simplified, soft shadows. Fix: none; entered as failure F9 for the owner with "accept" proposed. Status: open, for the owner.
- Whole phase, no owner photographs and no site visit. Fix (workaround): "PLACEHOLDER: no photograph" panels; 9 of 19 tolerances entered as "could not be checked" with the missing evidence named. Status: waits on the owner.
- Whole phase, no advisor tool in either session. Fix: the closing check of the changed files against the scope was done by hand. Status: reported to the owner.
- Closing, the working copy still shows `profiles\2026-11-12_M6\memory_log_phase8.csv` as changed. Cause: the memory log process of Phase 8 (process 30032) still appends to it. Fix: left alone, not this phase's process. Status: reported to the owner.

### Paused for the 5-hour usage limit -- 2026-11-13 08:47 (supervising session)

- Owner request: pause at a good point and restart when the 5-hour limit resets. The limit was 89 percent used and resets at 10:10 local (16:10 UTC). The weekly Fable limit was 83 percent used, resetting 2026-11-19.
- How: a `STOP` file in the watch folder now holds a resume time (`2026-11-13 10:15`). `keepalive.ps1` was extended to honour it: at that time it removes the file and starts the watcher, which relaunches the open phase. The pause survives a Windows restart, because the keep-alive starts at sign-in. The watcher was stopped at 08:47. `stop_at_commit.ps1` stops the Phase 10 agent at its next journal commit, so it ends at a recorded step.
- Status: paused; update when the resume is seen.

- Error: the Phase 13 agent stopped at 13:09 with "You're out of usage credits". The weekly Fable limit was at 100 percent and resets 2026-11-19 08:00 local. The 5-hour limit was at 90 percent; the weekly all-models limit at 59 percent.
